### MidPO: Dual Preference Optimization for Safety and Helpfulness in Large Language Models via a Mixture of Experts Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02460v1)
- **Authors**: Yupeng Qi, Ziyu Lyu, Min Yang, Yanlin Wang, Lu Bai, Lixin Cui
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) are increasingly applied across various domains, enhancing safety while maintaining the helpfulness of LLMs has become a critical challenge. Recent studies solve this problem through safety-constrained online preference optimization or safety-constrained offline preference optimization. However, the safety-constrained online methods often suffer from excessive safety, which might reduce helpfulness, while the safety-constrained offline methods perform poorly in adaptively balancing safety and helpfulness. To address these limitations, we propose MidPO, a \textbf{\underline{Mi}}xture of Experts (MoE) framework for safety-helpfulness \textbf{\underline{d}}ual \textbf{\underline{P}}reference \textbf{\underline{O}}ptimization. Firstly, MidPO devises single-preference enhanced direct preference optimization approach to transform the base model into two independent experts, termed safety and helpfulness experts, and fine-tunes the two independent experts for optimal safety or helpfulness performance. Secondly, to achieve an effective balance between safety and helpfulness, MidPO incorporates the two experts into the MoE framework and designs a dynamic routing mechanism to allocate contributions from each expert adaptively. We conduct quantitative and qualitative experiments on three popular datasets to demonstrate the proposed MidPO significantly outperforms state-of-the-art approaches in both safety and helpfulness. The code and models will be released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MidPO: Dual Preference Optimization for Safety and Helpfulness in Large Language Models via a Mixture of Experts Framework":

**Summary**

The paper introduces MidPO, a novel Mixture of Experts (MoE) framework designed to improve both safety and helpfulness in large language models (LLMs). Recognizing the limitations of existing approaches – safety-constrained online alignment potentially leading to over-refusal, and offline methods struggling to balance safety and helpfulness adaptively – MidPO proposes a two-pronged approach:

1.  **Single-Preference Enhanced Experts:** The base model is transformed into two specialized experts (safety and helpfulness) using a single-preference enhanced direct preference optimization (SPE-DPO) technique. This involves LoRA fine-tuning to optimize each expert for its respective single preference.
2.  **Dynamic Routing Mechanism:**  The LoRA weights of these experts are then integrated into an MoE framework. A dynamic routing mechanism is used to adaptively allocate contributions from each expert based on the input prompt. This allows the model to prioritize safety or helpfulness depending on the situation.

The authors present quantitative and qualitative experimental results on standard datasets that demonstrate MidPO's superior performance in balancing safety and helpfulness compared to state-of-the-art methods. The code and models will be released.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach by combining several existing techniques in a unique way. The individual components – MoE, LoRA, DPO – are not entirely new, but their synergistic combination to specifically address the safety-helpfulness trade-off is a contribution. The SPE-DPO and the dynamic routing mechanism appear to be the most novel aspects.  The dynamic routing mechanism, in particular, seems well-motivated to adaptively balance between safety and helpfulness experts.

**Significance:** Improving the safety and helpfulness of LLMs is a critical challenge for their widespread adoption and beneficial use. The paper directly tackles this problem, making it relevant to the field.  The experimental results, if robust and generalizable, show a practical improvement over existing methods which is significant.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and motivates the need for a new solution.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** The MidPO framework is clearly explained, with detailed descriptions of its components.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents both quantitative and qualitative evidence of MidPO's effectiveness. The use of several benchmark datasets and evaluations using GPT-4 and Deepseek-V3 enhances the validity of the results.
*   **Release of Code and Models:**  Making the code and models available will allow others to reproduce and build upon this work.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** As mentioned above, the individual components of MidPO are not entirely novel. The paper might be perceived as an incremental improvement rather than a groundbreaking innovation.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance of MoE-based models is often sensitive to hyperparameters. A more thorough analysis of the sensitivity of MidPO's performance to various hyperparameters would be beneficial. Also, justifying the use of L1 regularization for ensuring the helpfulness preference is also optimized simulataneously needs more details.
*   **Limited Evaluation of Computational Overhead:** While the inference time increase is acknowledged, a more detailed analysis of the computational cost of training MidPO (especially with the MoE architecture) would strengthen the paper. The statement of only having a "marginal increase" isn't descriptive enough.
*   **Generalizability:** There is no discussion on the paper about the generalizability to more advanced LLM models such as GPT-4 and Gemini.

**Potential Influence:**

If the experimental results hold up to further scrutiny and the implementation is relatively straightforward, MidPO could influence the development of future LLM alignment methods. Its adaptive approach to balancing safety and helpfulness could serve as a valuable blueprint. The main significance is providing a novel algorithm for safety alignment that enhances both the safety and helpfulness as opposed to other baseline methods that do not provide high level safety.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a well-executed application of existing techniques to address a significant problem in LLM alignment. While the individual components are not revolutionary, their combination and the resulting performance improvements warrant a solid score. It is not a groundbreaking theoretical contribution, but it is a practical and valuable engineering solution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### XToM: Exploring the Multilingual Theory of Mind for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02461v1)
- **Authors**: Chunkit Chan, Yauwai Yim, Hongchuan Zeng, Zhiying Zou, Xinyuan Cheng, Zhifan Sun, Zheye Deng, Kawai Chung, Yuzhuo Ao, Yixiang Fan, Cheng Jiayang, Ercong Nie, Ginny Y. Wong, Helmut Schmid, Hinrich Schütze, Simon See, Yangqiu Song
- **Abstract**: Theory of Mind (ToM), the ability to infer mental states in others, is pivotal for human social cognition. Existing evaluations of ToM in LLMs are largely limited to English, neglecting the linguistic diversity that shapes human cognition. This limitation raises a critical question: can LLMs exhibit Multilingual Theory of Mind, which is the capacity to reason about mental states across diverse linguistic contexts? To address this gap, we present XToM, a rigorously validated multilingual benchmark that evaluates ToM across five languages and incorporates diverse, contextually rich task scenarios. Using XToM, we systematically evaluate LLMs (e.g., DeepSeek R1), revealing a pronounced dissonance: while models excel in multilingual language understanding, their ToM performance varies across languages. Our findings expose limitations in LLMs' ability to replicate human-like mentalizing across linguistic contexts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, "XTOM: Exploring the Multilingual Theory of Mind for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces XToM, a new multilingual benchmark designed to evaluate the Theory of Mind (ToM) capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) across five languages: English, Chinese, German, French, and Japanese. Existing ToM evaluations for LLMs are primarily limited to English, neglecting the linguistic diversity that influences human cognition. XToM addresses this gap by incorporating diverse, contextually-rich task scenarios and rigorous human validation. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art LLMs (e.g., DeepSeek R1, Qwen3) using XToM and reveal a significant dissonance: while LLMs excel at multilingual language understanding, their ToM performance varies considerably across languages. This suggests that LLMs' apparent reasoning abilities may be superficial rather than deeply rooted in language-invariant cognition. The paper argues that XToM provides a high-quality resource for assessing LLMs' cognitive abilities, exposes limitations in their multilingual reasoning, and offers empirical insights into their performance across different linguistic contexts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation of a multilingual ToM benchmark is a valuable contribution. While the concept of evaluating LLMs on ToM is not new, extending it rigorously to multiple languages and incorporating human-validated translations is a significant step forward. Previous efforts have been limited in scope or relied on less robust translation methods.  However, the reliance on translating existing ToM tasks from English rather than designing tasks inherently suited to different cultural and linguistic contexts is a limiting factor.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights a crucial limitation of current LLMs: their potential inability to generalize ToM reasoning consistently across diverse linguistic settings. This has important implications for deploying these models globally, particularly in applications requiring accurate social cognition. The findings suggest that achieving human-like ToM in LLMs requires more than just multilingual language understanding; it necessitates a deeper understanding of cultural and linguistic nuances that shape social cognition. The XToM benchmark provides a tool for researchers to investigate this further. The MQM approach to ensuring translation quality is laudable and adds to the benchmark's credibility.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Multilingual Focus:**  The paper directly tackles the English-centric bias in LLM evaluation.
    *   **Rigorous Validation:** The use of the MQM framework and human annotators ensures a high-quality and reliable benchmark.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper evaluates a range of state-of-the-art LLMs and identifies clear performance discrepancies.
    *   **Task Diversity:** The integration of various ToM task scenarios adds depth to the evaluation.
    *   **Transparency:** The availability of code and data promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Translation Limitations:** While human-validated, translating existing English ToM tasks might not fully capture the nuances and cultural contexts of each target language. A more ambitious approach would involve designing ToM tasks *directly* in each language, reflecting culturally specific scenarios.
    *   **Limited Language Scope:** While covering five languages is a good start, expanding the benchmark to include more typologically diverse languages would increase its generalizability.
    *   **Task Complexity:**  The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of the inherent complexity and biases within each ToM task and how these might interact with different languages. Are certain tasks easier to translate or more readily understood in some cultures?
    *   **Contamination Concerns:** The paper acknowledges potential contamination from pre-training data. While the contamination checks are reassuring, the possibility remains, especially for datasets like ToMi that have been around for a while.

*   **Potential Influence:**  XToM has the potential to become a widely used benchmark for evaluating multilingual ToM in LLMs. It can guide future research towards developing more robust and culturally sensitive AI models. The findings will also inform the development of techniques to improve ToM reasoning in LLMs, such as targeted fine-tuning or prompt engineering.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a solid contribution to the field by addressing a significant gap in LLM evaluation – the lack of multilingual ToM benchmarks. The rigorous validation process and comprehensive experiments add to the credibility of the findings. While the reliance on translated tasks and limited language scope are weaknesses, they don't diminish the overall value of the work. XToM provides a useful tool for researchers and highlights the need for more culturally-aware AI models. While the work is innovative, it does have areas that can improve and be explored in future iterations.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generative Perception of Shape and Material from Differential Motion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02473v1)
- **Authors**: Xinran Nicole Han, Ko Nishino, Todd Zickler
- **Abstract**: Perceiving the shape and material of an object from a single image is inherently ambiguous, especially when lighting is unknown and unconstrained. Despite this, humans can often disentangle shape and material, and when they are uncertain, they often move their head slightly or rotate the object to help resolve the ambiguities. Inspired by this behavior, we introduce a novel conditional denoising-diffusion model that generates samples of shape-and-material maps from a short video of an object undergoing differential motions. Our parameter-efficient architecture allows training directly in pixel-space, and it generates many disentangled attributes of an object simultaneously. Trained on a modest number of synthetic object-motion videos with supervision on shape and material, the model exhibits compelling emergent behavior: For static observations, it produces diverse, multimodal predictions of plausible shape-and-material maps that capture the inherent ambiguities; and when objects move, the distributions quickly converge to more accurate explanations. The model also produces high-quality shape-and-material estimates for less ambiguous, real-world objects. By moving beyond single-view to continuous motion observations, our work suggests a generative perception approach for improving visual reasoning in physically-embodied systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach for perceiving shape and material properties of an object by observing its differential motion in a short video.  It proposes a conditional denoising-diffusion model, U-ViT3D-Mixer, which is trained on synthetic object-motion videos to jointly infer shape (surface normals) and material properties (albedo, roughness, metallic-ness, specularity).  The architecture employs efficient local 3D blocks with spatio-temporal attention and channel mixing at higher resolutions and transformer blocks with global attention at lower resolutions. The model demonstrates an emergent ability to produce diverse, multimodal predictions for static observations, reflecting inherent ambiguities (e.g., convex/concave).  Crucially, it effectively utilizes differential object motion to resolve these ambiguities, leading to more accurate explanations and high-quality shape-and-material estimates for real-world objects. The core idea is that observing an object moving allows the model to better disentangle shape and material, a process analogous to how humans resolve perceptual ambiguities by physically interacting with objects.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel combination of ideas. The use of a diffusion model to jointly estimate shape and material is not entirely new (RGB-X does that for still images), but the extension to a video setting and the explicit leveraging of differential motion are significant contributions.  The U-ViT3D-Mixer architecture, with its hybrid approach to spatio-temporal attention, is also a notable architectural innovation, especially given the computational challenges of video processing at high resolutions. Training from scratch instead of fine-tuning pretrained models is another good decision that allows the model to produce multimodal output distributions.

*   **Significance:** The work has the potential to influence the field of computer vision and embodied AI systems. The ability to infer shape and material properties jointly is crucial for building more robust and reliable perception systems. The emphasis on modeling ambiguity and leveraging motion cues aligns with how humans perceive the world, offering a potentially more natural and intuitive approach to visual reasoning. The presented generative perception paradigm provides a path for developing vision systems that operate under uncertainty.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Joint Inference:** Estimating shape and material simultaneously is a strong point, as these properties are inherently intertwined.
    *   **Motion Exploitation:**  The successful exploitation of differential motion to resolve ambiguities is a significant achievement. This resonates with cognitive science and demonstrates a practical benefit of active perception.
    *   **Architectural Efficiency:** The design of U-ViT3D-Mixer allows for training directly in pixel space and handling high-resolution videos without excessive computational demands.
    *   **Multimodal Outputs:** The ability to generate diverse, plausible explanations for ambiguous inputs is a desirable feature for perception systems.
    *   **Qualitative Results:** The relighting results and motion disambiguation examples are compelling and support the claims made in the paper.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Synthetic Training Data:** The model is trained primarily on synthetic data, which may limit its generalizability to more complex real-world scenarios with different types of motion and more diverse materials.
    *   **Limited Motion:**  The restricted class of motions (rotations about a fixed axis) is a limitation.  Real-world motions are typically more complex.
    *   **Isolated Objects:** The focus on isolated objects is a simplifying assumption.  Scenes with multiple objects and cluttered backgrounds pose a greater challenge.
    *   **Quantitative evaluation:** Most existing benchmarks only measure shape or material estimation, but not the two tasks jointly. The current combined metric, built with mean angular error (shape) and SSIM (material) may not effectively capture the benefits of a joint model.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper makes important contributions, several limitations need to be considered. The synthetic training data and restricted motion class suggest that there is room for improvement regarding robustness and generalizability. Also, there is a lack of evaluation of the joint model. On the other hand, the architecture and the concept of using differential motion to resolve ambiguities are powerful. With further work on data and motion types, as well as joint evaluation metrics, this approach could have a significant impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Better De-raining Generalization via Rainy Characteristics Memorization and Replay
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02477v1)
- **Authors**: Kunyu Wang, Xueyang Fu, Chengzhi Cao, Chengjie Ge, Wei Zhai, Zheng-Jun Zha
- **Abstract**: Current image de-raining methods primarily learn from a limited dataset, leading to inadequate performance in varied real-world rainy conditions. To tackle this, we introduce a new framework that enables networks to progressively expand their de-raining knowledge base by tapping into a growing pool of datasets, significantly boosting their adaptability. Drawing inspiration from the human brain's ability to continuously absorb and generalize from ongoing experiences, our approach borrow the mechanism of the complementary learning system. Specifically, we first deploy Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) to capture and retain the unique features of new data, mirroring the hippocampus's role in learning and memory. Then, the de-raining network is trained with both existing and GAN-synthesized data, mimicking the process of hippocampal replay and interleaved learning. Furthermore, we employ knowledge distillation with the replayed data to replicate the synergy between the neocortex's activity patterns triggered by hippocampal replays and the pre-existing neocortical knowledge. This comprehensive framework empowers the de-raining network to amass knowledge from various datasets, continually enhancing its performance on previously unseen rainy scenes. Our testing on three benchmark de-raining networks confirms the framework's effectiveness. It not only facilitates continuous knowledge accumulation across six datasets but also surpasses state-of-the-art methods in generalizing to new real-world scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of limited generalization in image de-raining methods due to training on restricted datasets. It proposes a novel framework called CLGID (Continual Learning for De-raining with Generative replay and Interleaved learning) inspired by the human brain's complementary learning system (CLS), involving the hippocampus and neocortex. CLGID employs Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) to capture and store the unique characteristics of new rainy datasets, mimicking the hippocampus. The de-raining network, acting as the neocortex, is then trained using a mix of GAN-generated "memories" and current data, emulating hippocampal replay and interleaved learning. Knowledge distillation is incorporated to maintain consistency between new and existing knowledge. A similarity-based training acceleration algorithm further optimizes the training process. The paper demonstrates CLGID's effectiveness through experiments on benchmark datasets and showcases its ability to accumulate knowledge from multiple datasets and generalize to unseen real-world rainy scenes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of drawing inspiration from the human brain's CLS and adapting it to the domain of image de-raining is relatively novel. While the individual components, such as GANs, knowledge distillation, and continual learning, are not entirely new, their integration within the CLGID framework is innovative. The similarity-based training acceleration algorithm further enhances the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation in current de-raining methods: the lack of generalization to diverse real-world conditions. By enabling continuous knowledge accumulation and adaptation, CLGID offers a promising approach to building more robust and adaptable de-raining systems. The empirical results demonstrate significant improvements in both memory and generalization compared to existing methods. This has significant practical implications for improving the performance of computer vision systems in real-world environments with varying weather conditions.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Inspiration:** The CLS analogy provides a strong conceptual foundation for the framework.
    *   **Integration:** The seamless integration of GANs, interleaved learning, knowledge distillation, and the similarity-based training acceleration algorithm creates a comprehensive and effective system.
    *   **Empirical Results:** The extensive experiments on multiple datasets and de-raining networks convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of CLGID.
    *   **Scalability:** The authors have included two additional scalability variants, further validating their approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The CLGID framework involves several components, potentially increasing its complexity compared to simpler approaches. While the paper provides a complexity analysis, further investigation into the individual contributions of each component would be beneficial.
    *   **GAN reliance:** The framework's performance relies heavily on the effectiveness of the GANs in capturing the characteristics of each dataset. If the GANs fail to adequately represent the rain streaks, the de-raining network's performance might be compromised. The GAN training might also need more compute and memory.
    *   **Limited number of sequences:** While using a large number of datasets, the authors have primarily focused on one sequence. While this can be considered a minor weakness, more sequences would be helpful.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of image de-raining and continual learning for computer vision tasks. It presents a novel and effective approach to address a crucial limitation of existing methods, paving the way for more robust and adaptable de-raining systems. The CLS analogy can inspire further research into biologically inspired approaches for continual learning in various computer vision applications.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

CLGID presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of image de-raining by addressing the critical issue of limited generalization. The framework is well-motivated, technically sound, and empirically validated. While the complexity of the framework and dependence on GAN quality are potential concerns, the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses. The potential influence of the work on the field is substantial, as it offers a promising path towards building more robust and adaptable de-raining systems.

The approach provides a compelling and effective solution to the challenges of continual learning in image de-raining, demonstrating significant improvements over existing methods. CLGID offers a more biologically plausible method that encourages incremental adaptation while retaining previously acquired knowledge. The inclusion of similarity-based training and the efforts made to improve scalability are commendable, further strengthening the impact of the paper. Overall, the paper's innovation, thorough evaluation, and potential influence warrant a score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### FroM: Frobenius Norm-Based Data-Free Adaptive Model Merging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02478v1)
- **Authors**: Zijian Li, Xiaocheng Feng, Huixin Liu, Yichong Huang, Ting Liu, Bing Qin
- **Abstract**: With the development of large language models, fine-tuning has emerged as an effective method to enhance performance in specific scenarios by injecting domain-specific knowledge. In this context, model merging techniques provide a solution for fusing knowledge from multiple fine-tuning models by combining their parameters. However, traditional methods often encounter task interference when merging full fine-tuning models, and this problem becomes even more evident in parameter-efficient fine-tuning scenarios. In this paper, we introduce an improvement to the RegMean method, which indirectly leverages the training data to approximate the outputs of the linear layers before and after merging. We propose an adaptive merging method called FroM, which directly measures the model parameters using the Frobenius norm, without any training data. By introducing an additional hyperparameter for control, FroM outperforms baseline methods across various fine-tuning scenarios, alleviating the task interference problem.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "FroM" (Frobenius Norm-Based Data-Free Adaptive Model Merging), a novel method for merging fine-tuned large language models (LLMs). FroM aims to address the problem of task interference, especially prominent in parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) scenarios like LoRA. Unlike existing methods that rely on linear combinations of parameters or approximations using training data statistics (like RegMean), FroM directly measures model parameters using the Frobenius norm, without requiring access to training data.  It adaptively adjusts merging coefficients based on the Frobenius norm of task vectors and introduces a hyperparameter for controlling the balance between different models.  The paper evaluates FroM in various settings, including full fine-tuning (FFT) and LoRA, and demonstrates its effectiveness compared to baseline methods across different tasks.  For LoRA merging, an alternating optimization algorithm leveraging LoRA-type structures is also proposed.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the data-free approach using the Frobenius norm to guide model merging. While the Frobenius norm itself isn't a groundbreaking concept, its application *directly* to the model weights for data-free adaptive merging, particularly in the context of mitigating task interference in PEFT, is a contribution. It provides a practical alternative to RegMean, which has the significant drawback of requiring access to the inner product matrix of the training data – often unavailable in practice. The adaptive coefficient based on this norm and the hyperparameter 'k' provide some control, however, the method is primarily a parameter adjustment. The alternating optimization approach for LORA-type structure also adds some incremental novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is in addressing a practical challenge in LLM deployment: merging knowledge from multiple fine-tuned models, particularly in settings where training data is unavailable or sensitive. Model merging offers a way to consolidate expertise from various specialized models into a single, more versatile model. Task interference is a known bottleneck, and a data-free method like FroM offers a more accessible solution. The paper's empirical results demonstrate its potential in various fine-tuning scenarios (FFT and LoRA) and model architectures. A potential pitfall is that for the Qwen2.5 models, the method struggled, suggesting that the method may struggle where there isn't appropriate pre-training, or linear-mode connectivity between the underlying models. Also, it is unclear the extent that the method prevents catastrophic forgetting; while it may alleviate task-interference, but in merging models with conflicting objectives, it is unclear what would happen. The paper also lacks a theoretical justification of using the Frobenius norm to guide model merging.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Data-free:** Addresses a critical limitation of methods like RegMean by removing the need for training data or its statistics.
    *   **Adaptive:** Dynamically adjusts merging weights based on the Frobenius norm of model parameters.
    *   **Applicability:** Can be applied to both FFT and PEFT models.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Demonstrates strong performance in various experiments, including outperforming baselines in some scenarios.
    *   **Alternating optimization for LORA-type structure:** Proposes a solution for merging LORA modules.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** The choice of Frobenius norm is somewhat heuristic, with less theoretical grounding. The paper does not explain *why* the Frobenius norm is a good proxy for model relevance or task interference.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance depends on the hyperparameter *k*, requiring careful tuning, however the paper lacks a principled method to select k.
    *   **Inconsistent Improvement:** FroM doesn't consistently outperform baselines across *all* benchmarks, suggesting the need for further refinement or task-specific adaptation. While the authors claim that the selection of hyperparameter $k$ enables a well-balanced trade-off, it doesn't explain how one would derive the ideal setting of $k$ in real-world scenarios.
    *   **Qwen2.5 Results:** The method doesn't produce good performance for the models trained with Qwen2.5 data.
    *   **No catastrophic forgetting tests.**
    *   **Ablation Study on the Hyperparameter:** The ablation study in section 5.3, although helpful, doesn't provide rigorous analysis on which value $k$ would be suitable for various tasks, which may impact real-world application.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a practical and accessible method for model merging that could be adopted by researchers and practitioners working with LLMs. By removing the data dependency, FroM makes model merging more feasible in a wider range of scenarios. This could encourage further exploration of data-free methods for model adaptation and combination.

**Score:** 6.5

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and practical data-free approach for adaptive model merging that addresses task interference in LLMs. The empirical results demonstrate its effectiveness in several scenarios. However, the heuristic nature of the method, the reliance on hyperparameter tuning, and the uneven performance across benchmarks limit its overall impact. The limited theoretical analysis is also a factor.

Despite these limitations, FroM offers a valuable contribution to the field by providing a more accessible and practical alternative to existing merging techniques. It is a solid contribution that could lead to further research in the area of data-free model adaptation and combination. The score reflects the balance between the paper's novelty, practical significance, and the weaknesses related to theoretical grounding and consistent empirical improvement.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### BitBypass: A New Direction in Jailbreaking Aligned Large Language Models with Bitstream Camouflage
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02479v1)
- **Authors**: Kalyan Nakka, Nitesh Saxena
- **Abstract**: The inherent risk of generating harmful and unsafe content by Large Language Models (LLMs), has highlighted the need for their safety alignment. Various techniques like supervised fine-tuning, reinforcement learning from human feedback, and red-teaming were developed for ensuring the safety alignment of LLMs. However, the robustness of these aligned LLMs is always challenged by adversarial attacks that exploit unexplored and underlying vulnerabilities of the safety alignment. In this paper, we develop a novel black-box jailbreak attack, called BitBypass, that leverages hyphen-separated bitstream camouflage for jailbreaking aligned LLMs. This represents a new direction in jailbreaking by exploiting fundamental information representation of data as continuous bits, rather than leveraging prompt engineering or adversarial manipulations. Our evaluation of five state-of-the-art LLMs, namely GPT-4o, Gemini 1.5, Claude 3.5, Llama 3.1, and Mixtral, in adversarial perspective, revealed the capabilities of BitBypass in bypassing their safety alignment and tricking them into generating harmful and unsafe content. Further, we observed that BitBypass outperforms several state-of-the-art jailbreak attacks in terms of stealthiness and attack success. Overall, these results highlights the effectiveness and efficiency of BitBypass in jailbreaking these state-of-the-art LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "BitBypass: A New Direction in Jailbreaking Aligned Large Language Models with Bitstream Camouflage":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel black-box jailbreak attack called BitBypass that exploits the representation of data as bitstreams to bypass the safety alignment of Large Language Models (LLMs). Instead of relying on prompt engineering, BitBypass transforms sensitive words in harmful prompts into hyphen-separated bitstream representations, replacing them with placeholders in the original prompt. This camouflage is intended to trick the LLM into understanding the intent without triggering safety mechanisms. The authors evaluate BitBypass on five state-of-the-art LLMs (GPT-4o, Gemini 1.5 Pro, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, Llama 3.1 70B, and Mixtral 8x22B) and compare its performance against direct instruction and other baseline jailbreak attacks. They also assess BitBypass's ability to bypass guard models and generate phishing content. The results demonstrate that BitBypass effectively reduces refusal rates and improves attack success rates compared to direct instruction and some baseline attacks, indicating its potential to generate harmful content. The paper also explores the impact of different system prompt characteristics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using bitstream camouflage represents a fresh perspective on jailbreaking LLMs. Most existing attacks focus on prompt engineering or manipulating input tokens directly. Exploring the binary representation level adds a new dimension to the attack surface. However, the core idea is somewhat straightforward, and its effectiveness relies heavily on specific design choices regarding system prompts and word replacement.
*   **Significance:** Jailbreaking LLMs remains a significant concern, and any technique that unveils new vulnerabilities is valuable. BitBypass reveals a weakness in how LLMs handle alternative data representations and highlights the need for more robust safety mechanisms.  However, the practical impact might be limited if the attack is easily mitigated through more sophisticated input sanitization or improved handling of bitstream-like inputs.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper presents a well-defined attack method with clear steps.
    *   The evaluation is reasonably comprehensive, covering multiple LLMs, datasets, and comparison with baseline attacks.
    *   The inclusion of a phishing content generation experiment adds a practical dimension to the evaluation.
    *   The ablation study provides some insights into the key components of the attack.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty, while present, could be viewed as incremental. The technique relies on clever prompt engineering around a relatively simple concept.
    *   The mitigation strategies are not deeply explored. The paper mentions perplexity-based screening but doesn't offer detailed analysis or experimentation.
    *   The evaluation could be more robust. The exact implementation of baseline defenses could be further clarified, and more advanced attacks from the recent literature should be included to test BitBypass against the current state of the art.
    *   The paper doesn't offer substantial theoretical insights into *why* bitstream camouflage works, which hinders the development of broader countermeasures.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the development of more robust input filtering and validation techniques for LLMs, including better handling of different data representations. It might also inspire further research into attacks that exploit low-level data encoding methods. However, given its relative simplicity, its influence may be limited if simple defenses are effective.

**Justification for Score:**

BitBypass presents a reasonably novel approach to jailbreaking LLMs and highlights a potentially significant vulnerability related to data representation. The evaluation is solid, but the theoretical depth and exploration of mitigation strategies are somewhat lacking. The paper's influence on the field will depend on the ease with which it can be defended against and how broadly it can be applied.
