The paper introduces PIGEON, a novel system for open-set living need prediction on life service platforms (like Meituan), addressing the limitations of traditional closed-set classification approaches. PIGEON leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate unrestricted textual descriptions of user needs. It incorporates a behavior-aware record retriever to understand user preferences, aligns predictions with Maslow's hierarchy of needs to ensure human relevance, and develops a recall module based on fine-tuned text embeddings to link needs with appropriate life services. To make the system deployable in practical settings, the authors utilize instruction tuning to improve the performance of smaller LLMs. Experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate significant improvements in need-based life service recall compared to closed-set baselines, with human evaluation confirming the reasonableness and specificity of the generated predictions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Strength:** The paper's primary contribution lies in reframing living need prediction as an *open-set* classification problem. This is a significant departure from existing literature that treats the problem as a closed-set task, thus inherently restricting the possible need classes that can be predicted. This innovative approach allows the model to capture the diversity and complexity of real-world human needs. The fusion of LLMs with Maslow's hierarchy and user behavior data is also a novel integration of concepts.

    *   **Weakness:** While innovative, the individual components used (LLMs, embedding models, GNNs) are not entirely novel *per se*. The originality stems from the specific *combination* and *adaptation* of these techniques to the problem of open-set living need prediction. The novelty is therefore primarily architectural and applied, rather than a fundamental theoretical breakthrough. The historical records based encoder seems to be inspired by existing recommendation papers.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strength:** The ability to accurately predict living needs has substantial implications for personalized service recommendations on platforms like Meituan, directly impacting user experience and platform efficiency. The open-set nature addresses a critical limitation of current systems, enabling the discovery of previously uncaptured needs and allowing the system to respond to evolving user demands. Instruction tuning to enable smaller LLMs for deployment significantly enhances the practical value of the work, making it more feasible for real-world applications. The experiments are thorough, using real-world datasets and incorporating human evaluation.

    *   **Weakness:** The evaluation is limited to datasets from a single platform (Meituan) in two cities in China. This raises concerns about the generalizability of the results to other geographical regions and/or different life service platforms. The paper could have benefitted from a discussion on the scalability challenges and limitations of LLM inference in real-time scenarios, even with the proposed optimizations. While the authors provide ablation studies and hyperparameter analysis, there is a lack of deep analysis into the failure cases or scenarios where PIGEON's predictions might be inaccurate or inappropriate. What are the error types the model produces?

*   **Clarity and Presentation:**
     The paper is well-written and generally clear, with a logical structure. The figures effectively illustrate the system architecture and experimental results.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by reframing a practical problem (living need prediction) within an open-set classification framework, leveraging the power of LLMs in conjunction with structured knowledge and behavioral data. The system shows considerable practical potential and achieves superior performance over existing approaches. However, the reliance on existing techniques and the limited scope of the evaluation dataset temper its overall impact. The significance of the work could be improved with more discussions on the limitations, error modes and the deployment strategies.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Heterogeneous Group-Based Reinforcement Learning for LLM-based Multi-Agent Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02718v1)
- **Authors**: Guanzhong Chen, Shaoxiong Yang, Chao Li, Wei Liu, Jian Luan, Zenglin Xu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable success across diverse natural language processing tasks, yet their deployment in real-world applications is hindered by fixed knowledge cutoffs and difficulties in generating controllable, accurate outputs in a single inference. Multi-agent systems (MAS) built from specialized LLM agents offer a promising solution, enabling dynamic collaboration and iterative reasoning. However, optimizing these systems remains a challenge, as conventional methods such as prompt engineering and supervised fine-tuning entail high engineering overhead and limited adaptability. Reinforcement learning (RL), particularly multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL), provides a scalable framework by refining agent policies based on system-level feedback. Nevertheless, existing MARL algorithms, such as Multi-Agent Proximal Policy Optimization (MAPPO), rely on Critic networks, which can cause training instability and increase computational burden. To address these limitations and target the prototypical Multi-Agent Search System (MASS), we propose Multi-Agent Heterogeneous Group Policy Optimization (MHGPO), a novel Critic-free algorithm that guides policy updates by estimating relative reward advantages across heterogeneous groups of rollouts. MHGPO eliminates the need for Critic networks, enhancing stability and reducing computational overhead. Additionally, we introduce three group rollout sampling strategies that trade off between efficiency and effectiveness. Experiments on a multi-agent LLM-based search system demonstrate that MHGPO consistently outperforms MAPPO in both task performance and computational efficiency, without requiring warm-up, underscoring its potential for stable and scalable optimization of complex LLM-based MAS.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Multi-Agent Heterogeneous Group Policy Optimization (MHGPO), a novel reinforcement learning algorithm designed for optimizing multi-agent systems (MAS) built from large language models (LLMs). MHGPO addresses the limitations of traditional MARL methods like MAPPO, which rely on Critic networks that can cause training instability and high computational overhead. MHGPO is Critic-free and guides policy updates by estimating relative reward advantages across heterogeneous groups of rollouts. The paper also proposes three group rollout sampling strategies (Independent Sampling, Fork-on-first, and Round-robin) to balance efficiency and effectiveness. Experiments on a multi-agent LLM-based search system (MASS) demonstrate that MHGPO outperforms MAPPO in task performance and computational efficiency, without requiring warm-up.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the application of Group-based Optimization Algorithms (GOAs), previously used in single-agent RL, to the multi-agent setting, specifically for LLM-based MAS. The MHGPO algorithm, along with its rollout sampling strategies, is a new contribution to the MARL field, particularly tailored for LLM agents. The critic-free approach is a significant departure from typical MARL methods and offers practical advantages in terms of stability and efficiency. The idea of leveraging heterogeneous groups to estimate relative rewards is also innovative.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in deploying LLMs in real-world applications: the need for controllable and accurate outputs. By optimizing MAS with RL, the paper offers a scalable framework for refining agent policies and improving system-level performance. The focus on MASS (multi-agent search systems) is also significant, as it is a prototypical instantiation of MASs and has numerous applications. The results demonstrate practical improvements in both task performance and computational efficiency, suggesting that MHGPO could be a valuable tool for optimizing complex LLM-based systems.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation. The paper clearly articulates the challenges of optimizing LLM-based MAS and motivates the need for a new RL algorithm.
    *   Novel algorithm design. MHGPO offers a critic-free approach, which is more stable and computationally efficient than traditional MARL methods.
    *   Comprehensive experiments. The paper includes thorough experiments on a multi-agent search system, comparing MHGPO with MAPPO and demonstrating its superior performance.
    *   Well-written and organized. The paper is well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the algorithm and experimental setup.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited scope. The experiments are focused on a single application (MASS) with a specific three agent setup. While the method is applicable to other MASs, more diverse experiments would strengthen the claims of generalizability.
    *   Relatively simple experimental setup.  The experiment doesn't explore more sophisticated, recent multi-agent environments such as those found in the social sciences, or environments with non-differentiable rewards. This limits the ability to generalize the claim about MHGPO's improvement.
    *   The theoretical underpinning of why specific rollout sampling strategies work is not fully fleshed out; it's primarily empirically driven.
    *   While MHGPO is shown to be more computationally efficient than MAPPO, a more detailed analysis of the computational complexity of different rollout sampling strategies would be beneficial.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of MARL for LLM-based MAS. The critic-free approach and the use of heterogeneous group rollouts offer practical advantages in terms of stability and efficiency. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of MHGPO on a multi-agent search system. However, the paper has some limitations in terms of scope and diversity of experiments. Addressing these limitations in future work would further strengthen the claims of the paper.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Benchmarking and Advancing Large Language Models for Local Life Services
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02720v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaochong Lan, Jie Feng, Jiahuan Lei, Xinlei Shi, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have exhibited remarkable capabilities and achieved significant breakthroughs across various domains, leading to their widespread adoption in recent years. Building on this progress, we investigate their potential in the realm of local life services. In this study, we establish a comprehensive benchmark and systematically evaluate the performance of diverse LLMs across a wide range of tasks relevant to local life services. To further enhance their effectiveness, we explore two key approaches: model fine-tuning and agent-based workflows. Our findings reveal that even a relatively compact 7B model can attain performance levels comparable to a much larger 72B model, effectively balancing inference cost and model capability. This optimization greatly enhances the feasibility and efficiency of deploying LLMs in real-world online services, making them more practical and accessible for local life applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Benchmarking and Advancing Large Language Models for Local Life Services":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the gap in evaluating and improving Large Language Models (LLMs) for the specific domain of "local life services" (e.g., services provided by Meituan, a platform similar to DoorDash or Yelp in China). The authors introduce LocalEval, a new benchmark specifically designed to assess LLM performance across a variety of tasks relevant to this domain. They also propose a novel multi-agent instruction synthesis method (LocalInstruction) to improve LLMs' understanding of local life services. Finally, they design agentic workflows for complex tasks, integrating expert knowledge to guide LLM reasoning.  Experiments show that fine-tuning smaller LLMs using LocalInstruction can achieve comparable performance to much larger models and that the proposed approach has a practical impact in real-world deployments within Meituan.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the creation of LocalEval. Existing LLM benchmarks tend to focus on general knowledge, common sense reasoning, or other specific domains like coding or medicine. The focus on local life services, with its emphasis on spatiotemporal contexts, user preferences, and complex interactions, is a relatively unexplored area. The multi-agent instruction synthesis method is also a valuable contribution, particularly in its focus on utilizing readily available platform data to create training examples. The use of agentic workflows to guide LLM reasoning on composite tasks is another positive.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to improve LLMs' capabilities in a domain with significant real-world impact. Local life service platforms are critical components of modern urban life. Enhancing LLM performance in this area can lead to improved recommendations, search results, user experiences, and ultimately, more efficient allocation of resources. Given the growing reliance on such services globally, any performance boost is significant. The demonstration that a smaller, fine-tuned model can match a larger one is also valuable from a cost-effectiveness and deployability perspective.  The real-world A/B test results from Meituan are a strong validation of practical value.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Defined Benchmark:** LocalEval is a comprehensive and well-structured benchmark with a clear focus on relevant tasks. The four categories – Service Fundamentals, Service with Context, User-Service Interaction, and Composite Tasks – cover a broad range of essential functionalities for LLMs.
    *   **Practical Approach:**  The LocalInstruction method leverages existing platform data, making it highly practical. The multi-agent approach is well-motivated and intelligently designed.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments are thorough, including comparisons to a wide variety of LLMs, ablation studies, and cross-city evaluations. The real-world deployment results further strengthen the credibility of the approach.
    *   **Code and Data Availability:** The authors provide links to their resources, promoting reproducibility and enabling further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Geographic Specificity:** The paper's focus on Meituan, and therefore, Chinese local life services, raises questions about the generalizability of the findings. While the core concepts (spatiotemporal context, user preferences) are universal, the specific tasks, data, and cultural nuances might limit the direct applicability of LocalEval to other regions. The authors acknowledge this limitation and conduct some cross-city validation, which is helpful but not fully conclusive.
    *   **Limited Hyperparameter Tuning details:** The paper mentions that hyperparameter tuning was not a focus. While they claim data quality is more important, exploration of this area may potentially yield more optimal results.
    *   **Incremental improvements, reliance on prompting:** While the results in real world deployment are strong, the increase in accuracy in the benchmark isn't very large in absolute terms (around 2%). This may reflect that a good instruction tuning dataset is enough without complex modelling methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several ways:

    *   **Spurring Further Research on LLMs for Local Life Services:** LocalEval will serve as a valuable resource for researchers interested in this domain.
    *   **Inspiring New Benchmarks for Other Domains:** The approach of creating domain-specific benchmarks can be applied to other areas where general-purpose LLMs may not be sufficient.
    *   **Promoting Data-Efficient Fine-tuning Methods:** The multi-agent instruction synthesis method offers a practical way to improve LLM performance with limited data.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** The paper makes a significant contribution by addressing an important gap in the LLM field: the evaluation and improvement of LLMs for local life services. The creation of LocalEval, a relevant benchmark, is a valuable contribution as it can drive further progress. The multi-agent approach to instruction tuning is also novel and effective. The demonstration of real-world impact further increases the paper's significance.

However, the geographic specificity of the data and the potential limitations on generalization prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. Furthermore, the lack of exploration on better performing prompts also limits its value. The improvements on the benchmark in absolute terms, aren't as large as would be expected with such complex modelling methods.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### RACE-Align: Retrieval-Augmented and Chain-of-Thought Enhanced Preference Alignment for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02726v1)
- **Authors**: Qihang Yan, Xinyu Zhang, Luming Guo, Qi Zhang, Feifan Liu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) struggle with accuracy, domain-specific reasoning, and interpretability in vertical domains. Traditional preference alignment methods like Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) often overlook the underlying knowledge sources and reasoning logic. This paper introduces RACE-Align (Retrieval-Augmented and Chain-of-Thought Enhanced Alignment), a novel framework designed to address these limitations. RACE-Align systematically constructs a binary preference dataset incorporating external knowledge support and explicit Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning, then aligns LLMs using the DPO algorithm. The core innovation lies in its preference data construction strategy: it integrates AI-driven retrieval for factual grounding, enhancing knowledgeability and accuracy, and emphasizes the optimization of domain-specific CoT, treating the reasoning process itself as a key preference dimension. A multi-stage, AI-driven refinement pipeline cost-effectively generates these preference pairs. Experimental validation in Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) using Qwen3-1.7B as the base model demonstrates that RACE-Align significantly outperforms the original base model and a model fine-tuned only with Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT). Improvements were observed across multiple dimensions, including answer accuracy, information richness, application of TCM thinking patterns, logicality and depth of reasoning, and interpretability. These findings suggest RACE-Align offers an effective pathway to enhance LLMs' knowledge application, reasoning reliability, and process transparency in complex vertical domains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RACE-Align, a novel framework for enhancing Large Language Models (LLMs) in vertical domains by integrating Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning, and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO). RACE-Align constructs a binary preference dataset that incorporates external knowledge and explicit CoT reasoning, then aligns LLMs using DPO. The key innovation is the preference data construction strategy, which integrates AI-driven retrieval for factual grounding and optimizes domain-specific CoT. Experiments in Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) using Qwen3-1.7B show RACE-Align significantly outperforms the original model and a supervised fine-tuned model, improving answer accuracy, information richness, TCM thinking patterns, reasoning quality, and interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the *systematic integration* of RAG, CoT optimization, and DPO, particularly with a focus on *explicitly optimizing the reasoning process itself* as a key preference dimension. While existing works have explored RAG with DPO or CoT with DPO separately, RACE-Align emphasizes reasoning process as a core optimization dimension by combining retrieval and domain-specific CoTs. Also the AI-Driven method for the preference data generation is a significant contribution. While the components (RAG, CoT, DPO) are known, the *holistic approach and the focus on reasoning optimization* in vertical domains is what sets this paper apart.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in applying LLMs to vertical domains where accuracy, domain expertise, logical rigor, and transparency are paramount. The demonstrated improvements in the TCM domain suggest a promising pathway for enhancing LLMs' reliability and interpretability in complex applications. The multi-stage AI-driven preference data generation pipeline offers a cost-effective approach to constructing high-quality datasets. The systematic way of combining RAG, CoT, and DPO is an interesting approach and should lead to related works that extend RACE-Align.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation (accuracy and reasoning issues in vertical LLMs).
    *   Well-defined framework with a multi-stage data generation pipeline.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating improvements across several dimensions (accuracy, information richness, reasoning, interpretability) in a challenging domain (TCM).
    *   The AI-driven approach enhances efficiency compared to manual data construction.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The evaluation is limited to the TCM domain. Generalizability to other vertical domains needs further investigation.
    *   Reliance on the quality of retrieved external knowledge. The framework's performance is tied to the accuracy and relevance of the RAG system.
    *   The "black box" nature of the AI models used in the data generation pipeline (Gemini 2.5, Qwen3). More insight into the potential biases in the preference data would be valuable.
    *   The number of human evaluators (5) is relatively small. A larger pool of evaluators would strengthen the validity of the human evaluation results.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to enhance LLMs in vertical domains. The systematic integration of RAG, CoT, and DPO, along with the AI-driven data generation pipeline, is a significant contribution. The experimental results provide strong evidence of the effectiveness of the RACE-Align framework. While the evaluation is limited to a single domain and the reliance on external knowledge and potential biases in the data generation process represent potential weaknesses, the paper's overall novelty, significance, and solid experimental results justify a score that reflects a significant step forward in preference alignment for LLMs in complex domains. Also, it helps to open the black-box of LLMs through the emphasis on logical rigor and reasoning.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Why do AI agents communicate in human language?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02739v1)
- **Authors**: Pengcheng Zhou, Yinglun Feng, Halimulati Julaiti, Zhongliang Yang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have become foundational to modern AI agent systems, enabling autonomous agents to reason and plan. In most existing systems, inter-agent communication relies primarily on natural language. While this design supports interpretability and human oversight, we argue that it introduces fundamental limitations in agent-to-agent coordination. The semantic space of natural language is structurally misaligned with the high-dimensional vector spaces in which LLMs operate, resulting in information loss and behavioral drift. Beyond surface-level inefficiencies, we highlight a deeper architectural limitation: current LLMs were not trained with the objective of supporting agentic behavior. As such, they lack mechanisms for modeling role continuity, task boundaries, and multi-agent dependencies. The standard next-token prediction paradigm fails to support the structural alignment required for robust, scalable agent coordination. Based on this, we argue that two core questions deserve careful examination: first, given that AI agents fundamentally operate in high-dimensional vector spaces, should they rely on a language system originally designed for human cognition as their communication medium? Second, should we consider developing a new model construction paradigm that builds models from the ground up to natively support structured communication, shared intentionality, and task alignment in multi-role, multi-agent environments? This paper calls for a reconsideration not only of how agents should communicate, but also of what it fundamentally means to train a model that natively supports multi-agent coordination and communication.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper critically examines the prevalent use of natural language as the primary communication method in AI agent systems built upon Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors argue that this approach, while initially appealing for its interpretability and ease of human oversight, introduces fundamental limitations in agent-to-agent coordination.  They highlight a structural misalignment between the semantic space of natural language and the high-dimensional vector spaces where LLMs operate. This misalignment leads to information loss, semantic drift, and difficulties in maintaining state consistency.  Furthermore, the paper contends that the standard next-token prediction training paradigm of current LLMs does not inherently support agentic behavior, lacking mechanisms for role continuity, task boundary management, and modeling inter-agent dependencies. The authors propose a reconsideration of agent communication strategies and advocate for developing new model training paradigms that natively support structured communication and shared intentionality from the ground up. They highlight the need for models that emphasize structural coupling, executable semantic transfer, and internal state alignment, rather than merely relying on human-interpretable language.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its systematic analysis and articulation of the limitations of using natural language for agent communication *specifically* within the context of LLM-based agent systems. While the challenges of natural language communication in traditional multi-agent systems are well-documented, the paper provides a compelling argument that LLMs exacerbate these issues due to the inherent mismatch between their internal representation and the structure of language. The idea of protocol induced misbehavior is interesting, although the phenomena being described have been observed elsewhere in MAS literature in different contexts. The paper's call for a new modeling paradigm with native support for collaboration is a valuable contribution, although it's primarily conceptual rather than providing concrete algorithmic solutions.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it questions a fundamental assumption in much of the current research on LLM-based agents. If the authors are correct, the field is potentially heading down a suboptimal path by over-relying on natural language. The paper encourages the community to explore alternative communication mechanisms and model architectures that may lead to more robust, scalable, and reliable multi-agent systems. The theoretical analysis is particularly strong, providing a framework for understanding and quantifying the limitations of current approaches.
    However, the paper remains mostly theoretical. There are no experimental results to demonstrate the proposed limitations or evaluate the potential of alternative approaches. This limits its immediate impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-structured argument.
    *   Comprehensive literature review.
    *   Strong theoretical analysis of the problem.
    *   Thought-provoking discussion of design requirements for future models.
    * The paper identifies fundamental architectural disconnects between LLMs' and agent-to-agent coordination.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Lack of empirical validation. The paper's claims are primarily based on theoretical reasoning and anecdotal evidence.
    *   The proposed solutions are high-level and lack concrete implementation details.
    *   The mathematical formalization is somewhat limited and doesn't lead to new insights beyond the descriptive arguments.
    * The paper could better highlight some previous work in MAS and DRL that attempt to address some of the mentioned challenges.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:

    *   It could shift the focus from incremental improvements to existing natural language-based systems towards exploring alternative communication mechanisms.
    *   It could inspire the development of new model training paradigms that explicitly support multi-agent collaboration.
    *   It could lead to a more rigorous evaluation of LLM-based agents, taking into account factors such as semantic consistency and behavioral reliability.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper offers a valuable and timely critique of a prevalent approach in a rapidly evolving field. While lacking empirical validation and concrete solutions, its clear articulation of the problem and its call for a paradigm shift justify a score of 7. It's a solid theoretical contribution that could have a significant impact on the future direction of research, provided the authors or others follow up with empirical work to support its claims and explore the proposed alternatives. The absence of experiments is the primary reason for not assigning a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Exploiting the English Vocabulary Profile for L2 word-level vocabulary assessment with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02758v1)
- **Authors**: Stefano Bannò, Kate Knill, Mark Gales
- **Abstract**: Vocabulary use is a fundamental aspect of second language (L2) proficiency. To date, its assessment by automated systems has typically examined the context-independent, or part-of-speech (PoS) related use of words. This paper introduces a novel approach to enable fine-grained vocabulary evaluation exploiting the precise use of words within a sentence. The scheme combines large language models (LLMs) with the English Vocabulary Profile (EVP). The EVP is a standard lexical resource that enables in-context vocabulary use to be linked with proficiency level. We evaluate the ability of LLMs to assign proficiency levels to individual words as they appear in L2 learner writing, addressing key challenges such as polysemy, contextual variation, and multi-word expressions. We compare LLMs to a PoS-based baseline. LLMs appear to exploit additional semantic information that yields improved performance. We also explore correlations between word-level proficiency and essay-level proficiency. Finally, the approach is applied to examine the consistency of the EVP proficiency levels. Results show that LLMs are well-suited for the task of vocabulary assessment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores using Large Language Models (LLMs) to assess the vocabulary proficiency of second language (L2) learners at a word level. It combines LLMs with the English Vocabulary Profile (EVP), a resource linking vocabulary usage to proficiency levels. The authors evaluate the ability of LLMs to assign proficiency levels to individual words in L2 learner writing, addressing challenges like polysemy and contextual variation. The paper compares LLM performance to a part-of-speech (PoS)-based baseline, investigates correlations between word-level and essay-level proficiency, and examines the consistency of EVP annotations. The results suggest LLMs are effective for vocabulary assessment by exploiting additional semantic information compared to simpler methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its direct application of LLMs for fine-grained, word-level vocabulary assessment in L2 writing, explicitly handling polysemy and contextual nuances.  While previous works have utilized machine learning for vocabulary assessment and difficulty estimation, and others have used BERT for contextualized embeddings of polysemous words, the combination of LLMs with a lexical resource like EVP for proficiency level prediction is a distinct and potentially impactful contribution. Prior work often focused on overall essay/text scoring or relied on simpler lexical features or rule-based systems. The focus on in-context assessment rather than discrete word knowledge is also a strength. The work also makes the annotations publicly available which facilitates further research and reproducibility.
*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because of the potential to improve automated writing evaluation (AWE) systems.  Currently, AWE often lacks detailed feedback on vocabulary use. This work offers a pathway to provide granular, pedagogically relevant feedback to learners. Furthermore, the exploration of the correlation between word-level and essay-level proficiency is important for understanding how vocabulary proficiency contributes to overall writing quality. The consistency check of EVP annotations is also valuable as it helps to validate and potentially refine the resource.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is clearly explained and replicable.
    *   The use of a standard lexical resource (EVP) grounds the assessment in established proficiency levels.
    *   The experiments are well-structured, with appropriate baselines.
    *   The analysis is thorough, addressing different aspects of vocabulary assessment.
    *   The authors acknowledge and address limitations.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation data (OneStopEnglish) might be limited in scope or representative of diverse L2 learner populations. While additional datasets are used, the word-level annotation is performed only on OneStopEnglish.
    *   The study could benefit from a more detailed error analysis to understand the types of mistakes the LLMs are making. For example, are specific types of polysemy harder to resolve? Are there recurring patterns of misclassification?
    *   While the handling of multi-word expressions is discussed, a more rigorous evaluation specifically focusing on multi-word expressions would strengthen the paper.
    *   The computational cost of using LLMs for this task is not discussed, which is a practical consideration for AWE systems.
    *   A comparison against other state-of-the-art methods in automated vocabulary assessment, even if they do not directly tackle the same task, would provide a better benchmark.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a compelling case for using LLMs for vocabulary assessment. The findings are likely to influence the development of more sophisticated AWE systems, leading to more effective feedback for L2 learners. Other researchers may build on this work by exploring different LLM architectures, incorporating other lexical resources, or applying the approach to different L2 languages.
*   **Further Work:**
    *   Evaluating the LLM's performance on larger, more diverse L2 learner corpora is critical to validate its generalizability.
    *   Investigating the impact of different prompting strategies and LLM fine-tuning techniques is important to optimize the performance.
    *   Exploring the use of this approach in a real-world AWE system and evaluating its impact on student learning is the next logical step.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates a novel and promising application of LLMs for L2 vocabulary assessment. The use of EVP and the focus on in-context proficiency level prediction is a valuable contribution. While the study has some limitations, it is well-executed and provides a solid foundation for future research in this area. A score of 7 reflects the paper's novelty and potential significance, while also acknowledging the limitations and the need for further validation and refinement.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Rethinking Machine Unlearning in Image Generation Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02761v1)
- **Authors**: Renyang Liu, Wenjie Feng, Tianwei Zhang, Wei Zhou, Xueqi Cheng, See-Kiong Ng
- **Abstract**: With the surge and widespread application of image generation models, data privacy and content safety have become major concerns and attracted great attention from users, service providers, and policymakers. Machine unlearning (MU) is recognized as a cost-effective and promising means to address these challenges. Despite some advancements, image generation model unlearning (IGMU) still faces remarkable gaps in practice, e.g., unclear task discrimination and unlearning guidelines, lack of an effective evaluation framework, and unreliable evaluation metrics. These can hinder the understanding of unlearning mechanisms and the design of practical unlearning algorithms. We perform exhaustive assessments over existing state-of-the-art unlearning algorithms and evaluation standards, and discover several critical flaws and challenges in IGMU tasks. Driven by these limitations, we make several core contributions, to facilitate the comprehensive understanding, standardized categorization, and reliable evaluation of IGMU. Specifically, (1) We design CatIGMU, a novel hierarchical task categorization framework. It provides detailed implementation guidance for IGMU, assisting in the design of unlearning algorithms and the construction of testbeds. (2) We introduce EvalIGMU, a comprehensive evaluation framework. It includes reliable quantitative metrics across five critical aspects. (3) We construct DataIGM, a high-quality unlearning dataset, which can be used for extensive evaluations of IGMU, training content detectors for judgment, and benchmarking the state-of-the-art unlearning algorithms. With EvalIGMU and DataIGM, we discover that most existing IGMU algorithms cannot handle the unlearning well across different evaluation dimensions, especially for preservation and robustness. Code and models are available at https://github.com/ryliu68/IGMU.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges in machine unlearning (MU) for image generation models (IGMs). It argues that current image generation model unlearning (IGMU) lacks clear task discrimination, unlearning guidelines, effective evaluation, and reliable metrics. The authors perform an exhaustive assessment of existing unlearning algorithms and evaluation standards, revealing critical flaws. To address these limitations, the paper introduces three core contributions: CATIGMU, a hierarchical task categorization framework; EVALIGMU, a comprehensive evaluation framework with reliable quantitative metrics; and DATAIGM, a high-quality unlearning dataset. The authors use EVALIGMU and DATAIGM to benchmark existing IGMU algorithms, demonstrating that most struggle to handle unlearning effectively across different dimensions, especially preservation and robustness.  The paper makes its code and datasets available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper provides a valuable contribution by thoroughly examining the state of IGMU research and highlighting the shortcomings of existing approaches and evaluation metrics. While individual components like new datasets, task taxonomies, and evaluation frameworks exist, the combination and comprehensive nature of the authors' work presents a strong novel contribution.  CATIGMU helps to standardize and provide a more structured view of the IGMU task space.  EVALIGMU seems to offer a more holistic set of metrics compared to relying on isolated detectors and proxy-based assessments.  The curated dataset DATAIGM also addresses a key need in the community for robust benchmarking.  The empirical validation by benchmarking existing algorithms reveals concrete weaknesses in the current state of IGMU.

**Significance:** The significance of this paper lies in its potential to advance research and development in practical and reliable IGMU methods. The paper directly addresses critical issues preventing the widespread and responsible use of IGMs. By providing a more consistent framework for task definition (CATIGMU), standardized evaluation metrics (EVALIGMU), and a benchmark dataset (DATAIGMU), the paper sets the stage for more transparent, reproducible, and impactful IGMU research. The paper's findings also highlight the necessity for future research to emphasize robustness and preservation in unlearning algorithms, suggesting that current approaches primarily focus on achieving forgetting while neglecting other crucial aspects.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper provides a detailed and thorough analysis of existing IGMU algorithms and evaluation methods.
*   **Practical Contributions:** The proposed CATIGMU, EVALIGMU, and DATAIGM offer concrete tools and resources for the IGMU research community.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The benchmarking of state-of-the-art algorithms using the proposed framework provides strong evidence for the limitations of current approaches.
*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper effectively articulates the key challenges and shortcomings in the field of IGMU.
* **Transparency:** The availability of data, code, and models strengthens the value and impact on the community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination is compelling, the individual components are perhaps not breakthroughs in isolation, which lowers the score a little.
*   **Scope of EVALIGMU:**  The EVALIGMU framework, while holistic, still depends on proxy-based evaluation for some aspects (e.g., content detectors). The reliance on these detectors inherently limits the evaluation quality to some extent, despite improvements in detector accuracy due to DATAIGM.
*   **Complexity:** The proposed framework is relatively complex, which might require a significant initial investment from researchers looking to adopt it. However, the benefits should outweigh this drawback in the long run.
* **Limited Theoretical Insights:** The paper focuses primarily on empirical analysis and framework development, with fewer theoretical insights into the underlying mechanisms of unlearning in image generation models.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **8**.  The paper effectively identifies and tackles significant shortcomings in the field of IGMU. Its contributions of CATIGMU, EVALIGMU, and DATAIGM have a high potential to enhance future research and development efforts. The comprehensiveness of the analysis, the practical tools provided, and the empirical validation are all major strengths. The relative lack of breakthrough theoretical insights, and some incremental novelty in the dataset framework, are minor drawbacks that slightly lower the score, but the potential impact and strong validation are undeniable.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### Reuse or Generate? Accelerating Code Editing via Edit-Oriented Speculative Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02780v1)
- **Authors**: Peiding Wang, Li Zhang, Fang Liu, Yinghao Zhu, Wang Xu, Lin Shi, Xiaoli Lian, Minxiao Li, Bo Shen, An Fu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in code editing, substantially enhancing software development productivity. However, the inherent complexity of code editing tasks forces existing approaches to rely on LLMs' autoregressive end-to-end generation, where decoding speed plays a critical role in efficiency. While inference acceleration techniques like speculative decoding are applied to improve the decoding efficiency, these methods fail to account for the unique characteristics of code editing tasks where changes are typically localized and existing code segments are reused. To address this limitation, we propose EfficientEdit, a novel method that improves LLM-based code editing efficiency through two key mechanisms based on speculative decoding: (1) effective reuse of original code segments while identifying potential edit locations, and (2) efficient generate edit content via high-quality drafts from edit-oriented draft models and a dynamic verification mechanism that balances quality and acceleration. Experimental results show that EfficientEdit can achieve up to 10.38$\times$ and 13.09$\times$ speedup compared to standard autoregressive decoding in CanItEdit and CodeIF-Bench, respectively, outperforming state-of-the-art inference acceleration approaches by up to 90.6%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EFFICIENTEDIT, a novel method for accelerating code editing using large language models (LLMs). Recognizing that code editing often involves reusing substantial parts of existing code, EFFICIENTEDIT leverages edit-oriented speculative decoding. This approach effectively reuses original code segments while identifying potential edit locations through a single forward pass of the target LLM.  It then generates edit content using high-quality drafts produced by edit-oriented draft models and a dynamic verification mechanism that balances quality and acceleration. Experimental results demonstrate significant speedups compared to standard autoregressive decoding and state-of-the-art inference acceleration methods. The approach uses an iterative reuse-generate paradigm to significantly accelerate LLM-based code editing while maintaining output quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the specific way EFFICIENTEDIT integrates speculative decoding with an awareness of the unique characteristics of code editing tasks.  While speculative decoding is a known technique, its adaptation to code editing, incorporating code reuse and edit-oriented draft models, presents a valuable contribution. The dynamic verification mechanism, adjusting the verification threshold based on the target model's confidence, adds another layer of refinement. The Edit-Oriented fine-tuning for the draft model is also a valuable addition, specifically focusing the draft model on the areas of code that are being changed. While existing techniques like FastFixer incorporate similar principles of code reuse, EFFICIENTEDIT's combined approach and demonstrated improvements are noteworthy.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a practical and crucial challenge: improving the efficiency of LLM-based code editing. LLMs are powerful tools, but their slow inference speed can be a bottleneck. EFFICIENTEDIT directly tackles this by significantly accelerating code editing while preserving output quality. This can have a substantial impact on software development workflows, improving productivity and reducing costs. The experimental results showcasing significant speedups further underscore the practical value of the method. The generalization to function-, class-, and repository-level edits also increases the impact. Furthermore, by clearly defining the different phases (Reuse and Generate) and the mechanisms within each (Edit-Oriented fine-tuning, Entropy-Aware Dynamic Verification), the paper provides a clear framework for future research to build upon.
**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper accurately identifies and articulates the inefficiency of existing LLM-based code editing approaches.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The EFFICIENTEDIT method is described in detail, with a clear explanation of its components and their interactions.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The experimental setup is well-designed, utilizing diverse benchmarks and comparing against strong baselines.
*   **Significant Results:** The reported speedups are substantial and demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component.
*   **Addresses a Practical Problem:** Improves the efficiency of a very practical problem in software development (i.e. code editing) which is becoming increasingly important due to more LLM usage.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** The paper discusses hyperparameter tuning (specifically, *k*, the base entropy threshold) but doesn't fully explore its sensitivity across different datasets or model combinations. More discussion on how these parameters were selected would add value.
*   **Computational Resources:** The experiments were conducted using relatively high-end hardware (NVIDIA RTX 4090 GPUs). While this is common in LLM research, it limits the immediate applicability of the method to environments with fewer resources. A discussion of the resource requirements and potential optimization strategies would be valuable.
*   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While the case study provides some qualitative insights, a more in-depth analysis of the types of edits that are effectively accelerated (and those that are not) would further strengthen the paper.
*   **Dependence on the Original Dataset:** While the authors perform the fine-tuning of the target model on an edited version of their dataset, there is still a high dependence on the performance of the model.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty of the specific integration of speculative decoding with code reuse awareness and edit-oriented draft models, the significant speedups achieved, and the potential impact on software development workflows, the paper represents a strong contribution. However, the limited discussion of hyperparameter sensitivity and resource requirements, along with the lack of more detailed qualitative analysis, slightly detract from its overall impact.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Rethinking Dynamic Networks and Heterogeneous Computing with Automatic Parallelization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02787v1)
- **Authors**: Ruilong Wu, Xinjiao Li, Yisu Wang, Xinyu Chen, Dirk Kutscher
- **Abstract**: Hybrid parallelism techniques are essential for efficiently training large language models (LLMs). Nevertheless, current automatic parallel planning frameworks often overlook the simultaneous consideration of node heterogeneity and dynamic network topology changes, limiting their effectiveness in practical applications. In this paper, we address these limitations by modeling heterogeneous nodes within dynamically changing network environments and leveraging simulation-based strategies to determine optimal parallel configurations. Our approach enables fine-grained workload allocation tailored for heterogeneous nodes and complex network scenarios, achieving performance competitive with state-of-the-art methods under regular and stable network conditions. Additionally, we introduce a strategy pruning technique to rapidly discard infeasible parallel configurations, substantially reducing the search space and accelerating the search process through parallel execution within the simulator. Preliminary evaluations confirm that our method notably enhances training performance on heterogeneous nodes and demonstrates improved adaptability in complex, dynamic scenarios such as cloud computing environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Rethinking Dynamic Networks and Heterogeneous Computing with Automatic Parallelization":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of efficiently training large language models (LLMs) in heterogeneous and dynamic network environments.  It argues that existing automatic parallelization frameworks are limited because they often overlook simultaneous node heterogeneity and dynamic network topology changes. The paper proposes a new approach that models heterogeneous nodes within dynamically changing network environments and uses simulation-based strategies to determine optimal parallel configurations. The core idea is to view computation as mapping data and algorithms onto computational devices and communication as data transmission across network links.  The proposed framework integrates operator splitting and fusion, adaptive task scheduling via parallel branch-and-bound, and resource management tailored to heterogeneous and dynamic conditions. Preliminary evaluations using SimAI show performance improvements over mainstream frameworks. The key contributions include a multi-edge physical link abstraction model, a parallelized branch-and-bound optimization algorithm, and improved training performance in complex scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its integrated approach to addressing several practical challenges in LLM training simultaneously. While individual components (operator splitting/fusion, branch-and-bound scheduling, modeling heterogeneous resources) have been explored before, the combination and application to *dynamic* and *heterogeneous* environments offer a new perspective. The multi-edge physical link abstraction model appears to be a novel attempt to represent network connectivity more accurately. The parallelized branch-and-bound approach is also a valuable contribution, as it aims to address the combinatorial complexity of finding optimal parallel strategies.  However, the paper would benefit from a clearer explanation of how these components are integrated and whether the integration introduces any new theoretical challenges or opportunities.

**Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its focus on practical challenges encountered in real-world LLM training deployments. Heterogeneity and dynamic network conditions are significant bottlenecks in production environments. If the proposed approach can demonstrably improve training performance and robustness in these scenarios, it could have a substantial impact on the efficiency and cost-effectiveness of LLM training. The validation using SimAI is a good starting point, but more extensive experimental results on real hardware (especially at scale) would be needed to fully demonstrate the practical significance of the work.  Also, it's important to note that frameworks like PyTorch FSDP and others have started to incorporate features addressing heterogeneity, so the extent to which this solution leapfrogs or complements these existing efforts needs further clarification.

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical Focus:** The paper tackles a relevant and important problem in LLM training.
*   **Integrated Approach:** Combines multiple optimization techniques into a single framework.
*   **Multi-Edge Abstraction:**  A potentially more realistic representation of network connectivity.
*   **Parallel Optimization:** Addresses the computational complexity of searching for optimal parallel strategies.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Experimental Validation:** The reliance on SimAI is a limitation.  Real-world experiments on a variety of hardware setups are needed to fully validate the approach. There is also a need to evaluate the approach with a wide array of different network topologies and types of node heterogeneity.
*   **Clarity of Integration:**  The paper could be clearer about how the different components of the framework are integrated and the rationale behind the specific design choices.
*   **Comparison to Existing Methods:** While the paper mentions ALPA, AMP, Metis, and Galvatron, a more detailed comparison of the proposed approach to these and other relevant frameworks (including recent advancements in FSDP and other parallelization techniques) would strengthen the analysis. The performance gains need to be contextualized against existing, comparable, approaches. A more detailed specification of the hardware configuration used in experiments is necessary to provide context.
*   **Scalability Concerns**: Although multithreading addresses the scalability of the approach, more experiments are necessary to confirm that it still operates efficiently on large clusters of heterogeneous GPUs.
*   **Lack of analysis of convergence and training stability**.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field by highlighting the importance of considering heterogeneity and dynamic network conditions in parallel LLM training frameworks. The proposed techniques could be adopted and extended by other researchers and practitioners. However, the actual influence will depend on the extent to which the approach can be validated in real-world deployments and integrated into existing LLM training workflows.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and practically relevant approach to addressing challenges in LLM training. The integration of multiple optimization techniques and the multi-edge abstraction model are promising contributions. However, the limited experimental validation and lack of a detailed comparison to existing methods weaken the current impact of the paper. A score of 6 reflects the potential significance of the work, tempered by the need for more extensive validation and contextualization. The approach addresses an important area and combines existing techniques in a potentially novel manner, but the current presentation and level of validation leave room for improvement. More convincing evidence on real-world hardware is required.

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Rethinking the effects of data contamination in Code Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02791v1)
- **Authors**: Zhen Yang, Hongyi Lin, Yifan He, Jie Xu, Zeyu Sun, Shuo Liu, Pengpeng Wang, Zhongxing Yu, Qingyuan Liang
- **Abstract**: In recent years, code intelligence has gained increasing importance in the field of automated software engineering. Meanwhile, the widespread adoption of Pretrained Language Models (PLMs) and Large Language Models (LLMs) has raised concerns regarding data contamination and its potential impact on model performance evaluation. This paper presents a systematic empirical study to investigate the fine-grained data contamination on code intelligence tasks. Our study involves diverse representative PLMs, namely RoBERTa and GPT-2, and LLMs, namely LLaMA and StarCoder, covering three major tasks: code translation, code generation, and code summarization. We categorize contamination scenarios into four types according to the code intelligence practice, namely input-only, output-only, unpaired, and paired contamination settings, and construct corresponding experimental and control groups for exploration. Experimental results show that, under the pre-training, fine-tuning, and inference paradigm adopted by PLMs, even deliberately injecting paired contamination does not lead to significant performance overestimation. But direct inference or small-scale fine-tuning uncovers the contamination effects. In contrast, LLMs with pre-training and inference paradigm are significantly affected by the paired contamination. Apart from the above, other contamination scenarios have no impact on both PLMs and LLMs. Our findings challenge the conventional belief that contamination inevitably leads to performance overestimation, providing new insights into the evaluation and deployment of code intelligence models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effects of data contamination in code intelligence tasks when using Pre-trained Language Models (PLMs) and Large Language Models (LLMs). It categorizes contamination into four scenarios: input-only, output-only, unpaired, and paired contamination.  The study employs a diverse set of models (RoBERTa, GPT-2, LLaMA, StarCoder) and tasks (code translation, generation, summarization).  The authors find that the impact of contamination varies significantly depending on the model architecture and training paradigm. Surprisingly, PLMs are largely unaffected by most contamination settings, while LLMs are significantly impacted by paired contamination only.  They also find that fine-tuning PLMs can mitigate the effects of contamination.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic investigation of **fine-grained data contamination** in code intelligence.  Previous studies have primarily focused on sample-level contamination or have not considered the specific nuances of code-related tasks. The categorization of contamination scenarios (input-only, output-only, etc.) is a valuable contribution, as it provides a more detailed understanding of how different types of data leakage can affect model performance. The finding that PLMs are more robust to contamination than LLMs, especially with sufficient fine-tuning, is somewhat unexpected and contributes to a more nuanced understanding of these models' vulnerabilities.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical concern in the field of code intelligence: the potential for data contamination to inflate model performance.  The insights provided by this study can help researchers and practitioners to design more robust evaluation protocols and to choose appropriate models for their specific needs. The paper challenges the widespread assumption that contamination invariably leads to overestimation, suggesting that the impact is more complex and dependent on the model and training regime. This has implications for how code intelligence models are developed, evaluated, and deployed. However, the limitations outlined below lower the significance score.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Systematic exploration of multiple contamination scenarios.
    *   Use of diverse models (PLMs and LLMs) and tasks.
    *   Clear experimental design with experimental and control groups.
    *   Statistical significance testing to validate the findings.
    *   Reproducible methods for creating contaminated datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Contamination Scale:** The paper acknowledges that the contamination proportion is small. While justified by real-world scenario replication of benchmarks, the effects of larger-scale contamination might be different and warrant further investigation. This is a critical limitation because the degree to which contamination is observed affects how seriously the results will be received by the community. *This lowers the potential impact significantly.*
    *   **Perturbation Quality:** Reliance on manual perturbation is a weakness. While double-checked, this method is less rigorous than automated approaches and introduces the potential for unintended biases. Also, *the authors did not offer a comprehensive list of subtoken replacement rules, thereby impacting its reproducibility.*
    *   The limited experimental environment is also a weakness: All experiments for PLMs and LLMs are conducted on two Nvidia A40 GPUs with 48GB of memory for each. *Whether the study could be generalized into a more abundant computing platform environment should be considered.*

*   **Impact:** The study can influence how code intelligence models are evaluated and deployed. It highlights the need for carefully considering the potential sources of data contamination and for using evaluation metrics that are robust to such contamination. *The key findings of contamination not affecting PLMs as significantly as LLMs are valuable but not extremely groundbreaking*. As such, the paper serves as a guide for future research directions, especially around mitigation strategies and robust evaluation frameworks.

*   **Conclusion:** Although the findings are interesting and challenge prevailing assumptions about data contamination, limitations in experimental scope and methodology temper the paper's overall impact and potential for widespread adoption of its recommendations. The authors present valuable insights, but these could have been strengthened with more robust approaches.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** The paper possesses novelty in its fine-grained approach to analyzing contamination but falls short of achieving a higher score due to methodological limitations and a somewhat constrained exploration of contamination scale. While the results are useful, the potential impact on the field is moderated by these factors.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### ProcrustesGPT: Compressing LLMs with Structured Matrices and Orthogonal Transformations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02818v1)
- **Authors**: Ekaterina Grishina, Mikhail Gorbunov, Maxim Rakhuba
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) demonstrate impressive results in natural language processing tasks but require a significant amount of computational and memory resources. Structured matrix representations are a promising way for reducing the number of parameters of these models. However, it seems unrealistic to expect that weight matrices of pretrained models can be accurately represented by structured matrices without any fine-tuning. To overcome this issue, we utilize the fact that LLM output is invariant under certain orthogonal transformations of weight matrices. This insight can be leveraged to identify transformations that significantly improve the compressibility of weights within structured classes. The proposed approach is applicable to various types of structured matrices that support efficient projection operations. Code is available at https://github.com/GrishKate/ProcrustesGPT
- **Summary**: The paper "ProcrustesGPT: Compressing LLMs with Structured Matrices and Orthogonal Transformations" introduces a novel fine-tuning-free framework for compressing large language model (LLM) weights. The core idea leverages the orthogonal invariance property of LLM outputs, optimizing orthogonal transformations of weight matrices to improve their compressibility within chosen structured matrix representations (e.g., sum of Kronecker products, GS-matrices). The method alternates between solving a Procrustes problem to find optimal orthogonal matrices and projecting the transformed weights onto the structured matrix class. Experiments on OPT and Llama2 models demonstrate that the proposed approach achieves better compression performance compared to directly applying structured matrix representations without orthogonal transformations, and surpasses a similar method SliceGPT at a similar compression levels. The paper also explores several practical aspects of the optimization process and analyzes the connection to the SliceGPT method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its effective combination of orthogonal invariance with structured matrix compression in a fine-tuning-free manner. While structured matrix compression and exploiting orthogonal invariance in LLMs are not entirely new individually, the specific approach of using orthogonal Procrustes to precondition weights for structured matrix approximation, coupled with an alternating optimization scheme, is a novel combination that offers a practical advantage.

**Significance:**  The significance stems from addressing a critical challenge in LLM deployment: reducing the computational and memory footprint. A fine-tuning-free compression method is particularly appealing because it circumvents the computational overhead of retraining or fine-tuning, thus enabling faster and more accessible LLM deployments. The experimental results, demonstrating improvements in perplexity and zero-shot task performance compared to direct structured matrix approximation and baseline as SliceGPT.

**Strengths:**
*   **Conceptually sound:** The framework is based on a theoretically grounded understanding of orthogonal invariance and structured matrix approximations.
*   **Practical relevance:** The method addresses a significant practical problem in deploying LLMs.
*   **Clear presentation:** The paper clearly explains the method and presents the experimental results in a comprehensive manner.
*   **Competitive results:** The experimental results show that the proposed method achieves competitive compression performance without fine-tuning.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Limited exploration of structured matrices:** While the paper focuses on Kronecker products and GS-matrices, exploring other structured matrix types might yield further improvements.
*   **Incremental improvement:** Though it surpasses SliceGPT, the gains are not revolutionary and may not justify a complete change in the compression strategy.
*   **Lack of theoretical analysis:** While the method is conceptually sound, a more formal analysis of the convergence properties of the alternating optimization scheme would strengthen the paper.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the combination of novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of **7** is appropriate. The paper introduces a novel and practically relevant method that outperforms existing techniques in a fine-tuning-free setting. The paper presents an important result that could potentially impact LLM compression and deployment, it will be likely more valuable now that fine-tuning methods have been well established for low-rank adaptation of models. While the improvements may not be substantial enough to radically alter the current state-of-the-art, it's a valuable contribution to the body of knowledge on efficient LLM compression. The method's simplicity and fine-tuning-free nature, if widely adopted, could have important real-world impacts as well.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### TO-GATE: Clarifying Questions and Summarizing Responses with Trajectory Optimization for Eliciting Human Preference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02827v1)
- **Authors**: Yulin Dou, Jiangming Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) can effectively elicit human preferences through multi-turn dialogue. Complex tasks can be accomplished through iterative clarifying questions and final responses generated by an LLM acting as a questioner (STaR-GATE; Andukuri et al., 2024}). However, existing approaches based on self-taught reasoning struggle to identify optimal dialogue trajectories and avoid irrelevant questions to the tasks. To address this limitation, we propose TO-GATE, a novel framework that enhances question generation through trajectory optimization, which consists of two key components: a clarification resolver that generates optimal questioning trajectories, and a summarizer that ensures task-aligned final responses. The trajectory optimization enables the model to produce effective elicitation questions and summary responses tailored to specific tasks. Experimental results demonstrate that TO-GATE significantly outperforms baseline methods, achieving a 9.32% improvement on standard preference elicitation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TO-GATE, a novel framework designed to improve the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to elicit human preferences through multi-turn dialogue. TO-GATE enhances question generation via trajectory optimization, using a clarification resolver (trained with contrastive learning) and a summarizer. The clarification resolver is trained using Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to distinguish between good and poor conversational trajectories. The summarizer is designed to generate responses tailored to the specific task, improving the quality of the final generated response.  The framework is evaluated on standard preference elicitation tasks.  The paper also introduces a deterministic evaluation metric designed to mitigate position bias in the response evaluation, enhancing the stability and reproducibility of results.  Experimental results show that TO-GATE outperforms baselines, establishing a new state-of-the-art for preference elicitation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its integration of trajectory optimization, specifically DPO, within a multi-turn dialogue setting for preference elicitation.  While DPO and self-taught reasoning (STaR) have been individually explored, their combination with a clarification resolver and summarizer within the context of human-agent interaction appears to be a unique contribution. The proposed deterministic evaluation metric also represents a useful contribution. However, the core ideas of self-taught reasoning and DPO are already well-established, which reduces the overall novelty of the work.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance of this work is in enhancing the effectiveness of LLMs in personalized interactions.  Improving preference elicitation can lead to more tailored and relevant responses in various applications (e.g., recommendation systems, personalized assistants). The reported 9.32% improvement is substantial, suggesting a practical benefit. The deterministic evaluation metric is valuable, especially since the original approach had flaws.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper offers a well-defined framework with clear components (clarification resolver, summarizer, trajectory optimization).
    *   The use of DPO within the trajectory optimization framework is justified and well-explained.
    *   The deterministic evaluation metric addresses a known limitation in previous work and provides a more robust assessment.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate clear improvements over the baselines.
    *   The ablation study helps to isolate the impact of the clarification resolver and summarizer modules.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper relies heavily on simulated interactions using GPT-4, which may not fully reflect the complexities of real-world human-agent dialogues.  The reliance on simulated environments creates an uncertainty with regard to the overall effectiveness of the approach.
    *   While the components of the framework are novel in the specific combination, each individual element (DPO, STaR, LLM-based summarization) is based on existing techniques. The innovation, though useful, is incremental.
    *   The analysis, while thorough, could be extended to include a more qualitative examination of the types of questions generated and the effectiveness of the summarizer in capturing nuanced user preferences.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in the area of personalized LLM interactions. The proposed framework can be adopted and extended by other researchers to explore different optimization strategies and dialogue structures. The deterministic evaluation metric can also be used as a standard for evaluating future preference elicitation systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-engineered framework that combines existing techniques in a novel way to achieve state-of-the-art performance on a relevant task. The paper introduces a valuable improvement to preference elicitation via trajectory optimization. The proposed deterministic evaluation method is an added benefit. However, the reliance on simulated interactions and the incremental nature of the innovation limit the overall impact. The reliance on simulated data makes the experimental validation more of a "proof-of-concept" than a demonstration of robust real-world applicability.
